
He read it.

He did not read it with his eyes. He did not know what to call what he read it with. He read it the way he had read the archive entry in the green book at a table in Denvash, under a north window: once, and then again, and then a third time, slowly, checking each word against the one before it, before he let himself believe any of it.

It stayed. It did not go out, the way the word had gone out. It sat there in the dark behind his breastbone, quiet and plain, in the way a figure sits on a page once it has been written down.

*Wind-adjacent.*

He knew where it had come from.

He knew it at once, before he had finished the third reading, with the same certainty that had told him something had changed. It did not come as an argument. It came as a fact, like the river being cold.

It had come from the grey half. It had come from two months of mornings in the shadow of the east wall, at a canvas post, with a girl who would not tell lies to anybody she trained. It had come from watching her step a hundred times from the inside with his arms in the wrong place, and once from the rope. It had come from the drop under her backhand that was lower than she had ever asked him to go. It had come from the fourth exchange against Dessa, going across, into the closing space, in a way that was not his. It had come from the third exchange against Sarel, when he had stopped watching and moved without deciding, the way she moved. That was the thing she had been trying to teach him for two months, and he had thought he was only learning it.

He had been gathering it. He had been gathering it all along, mornings and bouts and the long minutes at the rope, and he had never had any word for what it was he gathered. Now it had a name, or the beginning of one. *Adjacent.* Next to Wind, but not Wind. Lira's, and not Lira's. Partial. Undetermined. Unknown.

It was not an answer. It was a page with more on it than yesterday's.

He sat on the cot for a long time with the notice there in the dark behind his breastbone, and did not write anything.

Then he thought about Lira.

He thought about her in her room two streets over, behind her green door that locked from both sides, with Torvin's chimney in her window. He thought about going there. He could see the whole of it, the way he had seen the whole of it on the night of the summons, when he had shut the Log on the kitchen table and gone up through the dark to the window at the bottom of the roof stair. The habit of telling had begun that night. He had been proud of it. He could go now. He could knock on her green door, three times, soft, and she would open it with her coat on and say *what happened*. He could tell her.

*I think I've got something of yours.*

He tried the sentence in his head and it came out wrong. He tried it another way, and it came out wrong again.

He had promised her on the step on the night of Dessa: *when I know what it is, really know, you'll be the one I tell first.* He did not know what it was. He knew what it was called, now, a little. He knew where it came from. He did not know what it did, or why, or how a thing could be collected by watching, or whether it would answer if he called it. He did not know whether it was a thing he had done or a thing that had been done to him.

And in the grey half, on the morning after he had named her right foot, she had stood in front of him with her hands loose at her sides and said *mine are mine*. *You don't keep my things.*

This was hers. Or some of it was. He did not know how much.

He took the Log up off the blanket and turned it over to the back, and wrote.

*Not yet. I'm not hiding it. I just can't explain it yet, not even to myself. If I tell her now, she'll help me work it out, and afterward I'll never be sure which parts of the working out were mine.*

He read it back.

He was not sure that it was honest.

He read it a second time and was less sure. It had the shape of a reason. He could see the shape quite clearly, and he could not see whether there was anything inside it. It might be true. It might be the kind of true that has its legs sawn short. It might be a boy on a cot who had been told once, plainly, in the grey half, *you keep everything till you're sure*, keeping one more thing till he was sure, and writing down a good reason for it in his best hand.

He did not cross it out. He drew a line round it instead, a box, so that when he came back to it he would know he had not been sure.

---

Then, because he could not sit still with it any longer, he got up.

There was a narrow space between his cot and the wall, perhaps two strides long and one wide, where his bag stood and the cold came in under the window. He stood in it in his stockinged feet on the boards. The brothers were asleep. Yeni was asleep, or was lying with her eyes shut and listening, which with Yeni was the same thing.

He thought about Lira's step.

Not the whole of it, with the touches and the backhand. Only the beginning of it, the part he had watched a hundred times from the inside and never once been able to see. The first drop of the hip, which started everything, a small dip and turn, so slight that from the inside it looked like nothing at all.

He did it.

He did it on purpose, slowly, in the narrow space between his cot and the wall, at half the speed Lira would have done it. He dropped his hip the way he had seen her drop hers. He turned the way he had seen her turn. And he put his foot down where he had seen her put hers.

It worked.

It did not work well. It worked so badly that if anybody had been watching they would have thought he was trying to step over something on the floor and had misjudged it. It was clumsy. It was slow. It was nothing like the drop under her backhand, or the going across against Dessa, which had come out of him whole and perfect and not his. But for a moment, for one step, the boards between the cot and the wall were very slightly less firm about how far apart they were. It was the width of a hand, perhaps less. The space between where his foot had been and where it landed was a little shorter than it should have been, and he felt it go short under him, like a stair that is not quite the height you expected.

He had done it. Not had it done to him. Done it. On purpose. He had chosen to.

He stood there afterward in the narrow space, breathing.

He was breathing much harder than one slow step in his stockings had any right to make him. His hip ached, the left one, deep in the joint, as if he had carried something heavy up a long stair on that side. It cost something. He did not know what, or how much. He only knew that it had cost more than it should have, and that he had paid it, and that he did not know yet what it had bought.

He did it once more, to be sure.

It was worse the second time. It was slower and clumsier, and the boards were only a very little less firm, hardly at all, and the hip ached more after it. He did not do it a third time. He sat down on the end of the cot with his hand pressed to the hip and his breath coming short. He turned the fact over in his mind like a stone he had picked up off a road and could not yet tell the weight of.

Then he picked up the Log and wrote under the box.

*I did it on purpose. Once. Badly, slowly, and it cost something in the hip and the breath that I can't measure yet. Twice, worse. But on purpose.*

*Every time before, it came when I didn't ask for it and wasn't there when I did. Tonight I asked, and it came. Not much. A little. Badly.*

*I think that's the thing that matters. Not that it's there. That it's started to answer when I ask.*

He read that over, and it was true all the way through, and he left it.

---

He wrote to Hesk before he slept, because it was Saturday, and the letter would go in the west bag on Monday and be in Denvash by the Monday after. And because he had found, sitting on the cot with his hip aching, that he wanted very much to write to somebody. There was only one person it could be, and he could not write it to him.

He wrote it short.

*Hesk,*

*Six weeks nearly done. Six bouts in them, four won. The last was a man everybody here uses to measure people by, and he put his hand up in the fifth. People were saying it in the street on the way out. Lira says that's the one they'll repeat.*

*Lira's drawn a line under the forty. I'll tell you about the forty another time; it's a long story and most of it is me saying one word.*

*Tell Joren I'm putting my hand on the ground at my mark before every bout now. He'll know why, or he won't, and either way he'll like it.*

*Tell Alis I laughed.*

*I'm all right. The ribs are mending.*

*— Cael*

He read it over. It was true all the way through. He folded it and put it in the leather book to keep flat for the morning.

Then he sat with the leather book closed on his knee and counted, as he always did now, what he had not told Hesk.

The cart. Renn. The drop. Dessa. And tonight.

It was five.

He sat with the number for a long time, in the cold, with the wheezing man whistling by the door. It was not that it had gone up. He had known it would go up the moment he read the notice; he had made a promise on a step about who would hear first. It was that tonight it had gone up and the other number had not gone down. There was one person who was meant to be first. She was two streets away behind a green door, and he had not gone.

He blew out his small piece of the light by turning his face to the wall. He lay a long time with the notice quiet behind his breastbone and his hip aching, and listened to the district's late sounds come up through the window, and did not sleep for a while.

---

On Tuesday the afternoon card went off without him, three bouts, and he stood at the rope and watched all three and wrote them down. Then the yard emptied, and the pie boy began to rake the circle, and the crows came down along the eaves, and he went to Vell's table.

She was finishing an entry. She did not look up until she had blotted it.

"Sit down," she said.

There was nowhere to sit but the dirt and the barrel, so he stood, and she did not seem to mind.

"Assessed-Copper," said Vell.

He waited.

"It's not a real rank. I want that said first, so you don't go thinking it is." She turned her tea a quarter turn by its handle. "It's a word I use. It means the circuit has watched you for long enough to say you fight at about Copper 5, or 6, on a good night, whatever the registry calls you. And the registry calls you nothing." She looked at the ledger. "Take it to Denvash and they'd not know what it meant. Take it across the river and they'd laugh. Nobody outside this district would give you a mark for it."

"But inside it," said Cael.

"Inside it," said Vell, "it's the difference between a boy people bet on for sport and a fighter people plan for." She picked up her pen and wrote. It was a short line, beside the long-standing one at the top of his page. He could not read it upside down, and she did not turn the book. "That's worth something here. It's worth more here than you know yet."

She blotted it.

"Is it worth anything to you?" said Cael.

He had not known he was going to ask it until it was out. Vell looked up at him. Something went across her weathered face, very nearly amusement, and was put away again.

"I don't rate fighters because I like them," she said. "I rate them because an honest book is the only thing that keeps this circuit from tearing itself apart over who's good." She looked at him a moment longer. Then she said, more quietly, in a different voice, the one she kept for things that were hers: "For what it's worth. I do."

"Thank you."

"Don't thank me." She went back to the book. "You did the fighting."

He went.

Lira was sitting on the low wall at the side of the yard with her knees up and her coat round them, in the last of the light. She had waited. She had not come to the table. She had sat on the wall, as she had sat on the river wall on the day of the six weeks, with the look of somebody who had been happening to pass a particular place for about an hour.

"Well?" she said.

"Assessed-Copper."

She did not jump off the wall. She sat quite still for a moment. Then the slow smile began, the one that started behind her eyes and took its time getting out.

"Copper 5," she said. "Or 6. On a good night." She got down off the wall. "That's higher than mine, you know. My circuit rating. You're past me." She did not sound as if she minded. She sounded as if she had won something herself. "I vouched for you. Three months ago, near enough, with my name on it, for a boy who said a word nobody had ever heard out loud. And now you're past me." She held out her good hand to pull him up off nothing, out of habit, and then laughed and dropped it. "Come on. It's the forty-second day. You've spent them all."

They went out through the gate in the wall together, and down the lane in the dusk toward the well with the iron cover, where she would stop, and go one way, and he the other.

He walked beside her and did not say it.

He had it with him. It was behind his breastbone, quiet and plain, in the dark place that was not quite as dark as it had been. *Wind-adjacent.* It was there every moment. It had been there at Vell's table and on the wall and in the gate, and it was there now in the lane. Every step he took beside her, it was there, and every step he did not say it.

The six weeks were spent. Somewhere on the merchant road, or at a desk two days west, somebody more senior than Coss was being found and sent, with a file that was too thin and a question nobody had told Coss. A girl with a green door was walking beside him in the dusk, pleased about a word that was not a rank. She had drawn a line under forty. She had told him, plainly, *mine are mine*.

At the well she stopped, as she always stopped.

"Same time tomorrow," said Lira.

"Same time tomorrow."

She went off up the lane toward the chandler's, and he stood at the well and watched her go until he could not see her. Then he turned toward Torvin's, two streets the other way.

He did not know how long he could carry it. He knew it would not be long. He thought, walking home with his hip aching, that it might be two weeks, if he was careful, and that he did not want to be careful. He thought that the thing behind his breastbone was partly hers. He thought that every morning he kept it from her in the grey half, it would get heavier, the way a thing does that has been held up too long. Nothing that has to be held is free.

---

## FORMULA ALIGNMENT — EDITORIAL, NOT BLIND

# O'Connor 1.3.0 — numerical author formula

# Applying the owner's numerical craft formula

The owner explicitly selected
`/Users/drive/penname/research/ironprince-craft-formula.md` to govern this book.
Use its craft instructions more or less exactly, including the numbers. These
are active design targets for the planning model and Opus/Fable author, not merely
editorial observations. The compiler embeds sections 2–6 and 8–9 verbatim below
these instructions and records the source hash so runs can be compared.

Let the sentence-length distribution, short/long sentence shares, paragraph
rhythm and Flesch reading targets influence the actual prose. Let the POV shares,
front-loaded progression vocabulary and distributed character-development rates
influence how the book is built. Preserve section 8's secondary/use-to-taste
status; do not turn its explicitly excluded filler/repetition tics into quotas.

Write original scenes in open-range movements of six or more chapters. The formula constrains the intended
shape and cadence; the author still chooses conversations, tactics, discoveries
and how events unfold. Do not reduce this brief to “clear writing with progression.”
The owner names the cast. No source novel quotations, character substitutions or
scene-by-scene replication belong in the manuscript.

Aim at the supplied distributions across a movement and the cumulative book.
Book-third teaching proportions and book-level POV proportions need not be met
independently by each chapter. Do not stop after each sentence or chapter to run a
gate. Write the full movement, then compare target versus observed results in the
existing editorial pass and make a focused same-author repair for material drift.
Do not silently broaden tolerances or drop inconvenient targets.

Keep the source's own caveats: pause-derived paragraph/scene measures are
directional proxies, and development counts are heuristic. Their adoption as
design targets does not establish that they accurately measure the printed book.
The full source-book audit remains unfinished. Keep source values such as ~1.7x
and ~950 words unchanged; the known arithmetic/denominator questions belong in
the audit, not a silent rewrite of the owner's chosen prompt. If competing targets
cannot be reconciled, report the conflict rather than fabricating a passing score.

This prompt guides output; it does not train model weights or guarantee exact
statistical compliance. Compliance must be measured after prose exists.

Source: /Users/drive/penname/research/ironprince-craft-formula.md
Source SHA-256: 9f97e2f225c0bcf61d2e922719153fcf00e3b029f7b1552f213888d33130c0b8

The following craft sections (2–6, 8–9) are included verbatim. Sections 1 and 7's legal discussion is not drafting guidance. Use original names, world, systems, events and phrasing; the owner names the cast.

## 2. Sentence rhythm

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Mean sentence length | 14.6 words |
| Median sentence length | 11 words |
| Spread (population stdev) | ~26 words — wide, meaning short declaratives sit right next to long compound-complex sentences, not clustered near the mean |
| Share of very short sentences (≤5 words) | 27.7% |
| Share of very long sentences (≥40 words) | 3.3% |

**Read:** roughly 1 sentence in 4 is a hard, short beat (often a single clause landing a
punch after a longer setup) — this is the "short sentence for impact" technique, not
uniform mid-length prose. Target this ratio, not just the mean; a manuscript that hits mean
14.6 but has no short-sentence spikes will not read the same.

## 3. Paragraph and scene rhythm (ASR-inferred proxy)

Whisper transcripts carry no typographic paragraph marks — this section is inferred from
pause gaps between narrated speech segments, not a count of the print manuscript's actual
paragraph breaks. Treat as directional, not exact.

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Inferred paragraph length (pause ≥0.6s) | median 18 words, mean 26.8 words — short, frequent beats |
| Inferred scene/section length (pause ≥1.6s) | mean ~950 words between scene-level breaks, ~8.7 scene breaks per 10k words |

**Read:** short paragraphs are the default texture, with scenes running roughly 900–1000
words before a beat change (POV settle, location change, or time-skip). Avoid long
unbroken expository blocks; break early and often.

## 4. Progression-mechanic ("change frequency") callout rate

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Progression/stat-vocabulary density | ~58 hits per 10k words (rank, growth-analog, spec, level-up, stat-block language) |
| Placement | front-loaded — the opening third of the book carries roughly 1.7x the progression-callout density of the middle and closing thirds (900 vs. 471 vs. 514 hits, by third) |

**Read:** the system gets *established* early and hard, then recedes into the background
as character and plot take over — it does not stay evenly dense through the whole book.
Build your own progression system's vocabulary (do not reuse his axis names — see §7), but
match this front-loaded shape: teach the mechanic aggressively in the first third, then let
it surface only at genuine turning points afterward.

## 5. Character-development beat cadence

Methodology: named-character mention within ~120 characters of a reflection/interiority
marker ("realized," "decided," "for the first time," "swore," "no longer," etc.) — a
heuristic count, not a scene-by-scene read.

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Protagonist development-moment rate | 4.5 per 10k words |
| Protagonist distribution across thirds | roughly even (41 / 56 / 50 — a slight middle-third bump, not backloaded) |
| Supporting-cast development-moment rate | an order of magnitude lower per character (0.1–0.7 per 10k words each), spread across 8–10 named characters rather than concentrated in one deuteragonist |

**Read:** growth beats for the lead land steadily throughout, not saved up for a finale —
and the supporting cast gets *many* small beats each rather than one character getting a
big secondary arc. If your story gives one best-friend character 80% of the secondary
development budget, that's a different shape than this book's.

## 6. POV structure

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Protagonist POV share | ~87% of chapter-word-volume (combining his given-name and surname references) |
| Secondary-character POV/focus share | ~13%, spread across 4–5 named characters at 2.5–3.7% each — brief cutaways, not sustained alternating-POV chapters |

**Read:** this is single-POV-dominant with occasional short cutaway scenes to a handful of
other named characters, not a true multi-POV structure. If you want to signal "feels like
Iron Prince," don't build alternating-chapter dual POV — keep the lead's POV overwhelming
and let other characters get glimpses, not chapters.

## 8. Supporting craft signals (secondary, use to taste)

- **Filler-word discipline:** "that" runs hot (92.1 per 10k words) — a known, common genre
  tic, not a target to imitate; if anything, tighten below this. Other fillers ("just,"
  "almost," "felt," "seemed") sit in the 5–25-per-10k band — moderate, not aggressively
  self-edited, not sloppy either.
- **Adverb density:** ~161 -ly adverbs per 10k words — fairly high; this is a book that
  lets adverbs carry tone rather than insisting on pure show-don't-tell. Not a craft flaw
  to correct, a genre-register signal to optionally match.
- **Dialogue-tag (reporting-verb) density:** ~41 per 10k words — moderate dialogue presence,
  consistent with an action/interiority-forward book rather than a dialogue-driven one.
- **Combat-vocabulary density:** ~22 per 10k words (strike/block/dodge/blade/impact/etc.) —
  present throughout, not just in set-piece fight chapters.
- **Readability:** Flesch Reading Ease 72.3, Flesch-Kincaid grade 6.8 — accessible YA/adult
  crossover register, short-to-medium sentence construction, low jargon-density outside the
  progression-vocabulary spikes.
- **Repetition discipline:** common connective 3-grams ("out of the," "one of the," "for a
  moment") sit at 2–4 per 10k words — normal English-prose background rate, not a
  distinctive tic to replicate. Don't chase these; they're noise, not signature.

---

## 9. One-paragraph brief for the drafting agent

> Write single-POV-dominant (~85–90% lead-character screen time), short-paragraph,
> pause-heavy prose (median paragraph ~18 words, scenes breaking roughly every 900–1000
> words). Mix short punch sentences (~28% of all sentences ≤5 words) against longer
> compound-complex ones (mean 14.6 words, wide spread) rather than settling into uniform
> mid-length sentences. Establish your progression system hard and often in the first
> third of the book, then let it recede to occasional high-stakes surfacing afterward. Land
> a protagonist growth/realization beat roughly every 2,200 words, spread evenly across the
> book rather than saved for the climax, and give 8–10 supporting characters small
> development beats each rather than concentrating growth in one deuteragonist. Moderate
> dialogue-tag and adverb use; accessible ~7th-grade reading level; combat vocabulary
> present in the background throughout, not confined to fight scenes. Every name, every
> system-label, every scene: yours, invented, original.

## NUMERICAL REVIEW

# Numerical formula alignment — after the movement

This is an informed editorial pass, not a blind cold read. Read the supplied
formula and full movement, then report target, observed result, method, coverage
and uncertainty. Keep numerical alignment separate from reader scores.

- Sentence mean, median, population standard deviation; shares ≤5 and ≥40 words.
- Written paragraph mean/median and actual scene/section boundaries. Compare with
  the requested targets, but do not equate typography with ASR pause segmentation.
- Flesch Reading Ease and Flesch-Kincaid grade. Identify the sentence tokenizer and
  syllable estimator; proper names/system terms can affect estimates.
- Progression vocabulary hits per 10,000 words, and placement by equal word-count
  book thirds once available. Define a project-specific lexicon before counting;
  do not borrow the source novel's names. An incomplete book cannot confirm the
  full front-loading ratio. Report provisional cumulative coverage instead.
- Lead development moments and supporting-cast distribution: cite actual choices
  or realizations and distinguish manually identified beats from regex proxies.
- POV word share: identify viewpoint segments, not the most frequently mentioned
  name. Track the lead and secondary viewpoints against the book-level allocation.
- Secondary signals: -ly words, reporting verbs, combat terms, fillers and repeated
  phrases. Publish lexicons and counting definitions. A suffix count is not proof
  that every matched word is an adverb. Preserve optional/non-target distinctions.

Use reproducible counts when tooling exists. Otherwise mark a metric UNMEASURED;
never estimate a precise score by intuition. Save the method or command with the
report so later runs use the same definitions. Inspect ASR reference limitations
without discarding the owner's chosen drafting targets.

Locate meaningful departures, especially uniform sentence lengths, missing short
beats, reading level drift, dense paragraphs, late teaching or a coequal second
lead replacing the requested POV shape. Consolidate the most important repairs
with continuity findings into the existing bounded author repair. Protect exact
owner text and successful scenes. Do not iterate indefinitely to hit decimals or
hide irreconcilable targets. Bring consequential formula conflicts to the owner.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — editions/monroe-1.3/EDITION_BRIEF.md

# The Fractured Path — Monroe Jackson 1.3 edition (O'Connor 1.3 seat)

Owner direction, 2026-10-01: rewrite the whole Fractured Path series through the
O'Connor 1.3 seat on the Monroe Jackson 1.3 foundation, with Claude Opus 5.5 as the
manuscript author, at about 300,000 words per book. When a book is complete, render
every chapter through Breeze and publish it as a new edition. The current edition
stays exactly as it is; this edition is new and recoverable.

Public byline: Monroe Jackson. Seat: `oconnor` 1.3.0. Foundation: Monroe Jackson 1.3.0.
Requested author: Opus (Claude Opus 5.5). Coordinator: this orchestrator session.
Review seat: Sol (gpt-5.6-sol via `codex exec`) — a different model family from the author.

## What "rewrite" means here

This is a retelling at full length, not a copy-edit. The prose is new. The story is the
same series.

**Keep (canon — binding):**
- Every book's spine: the major events, their order and outcomes, who lives and dies,
  the ending state, ages, the calendar where later books depend on it.
- Cael's fragment acquisitions — which fragment, in which book, under what engagement —
  and the counts at each book's close. The system notice texts that later books quote.
- The SECRET reveal schedule in `universe/CANON_RULES.md` and every reserved truth. A
  rewrite may plant more; it may never disclose earlier than the schedule.
- The universe rules, costs and limits in `universe/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md`.
- Relationships and their turning points as the existing books establish them.
- Lines a later book quotes or calls back (log entries, notices, signature exchanges).
  The book planner lists these as protected wording.

**Change (the point of the edition):**
- Length: about 300,000 words per book (owner). The existing books are 100–125k. The
  extra ~190k comes from: developed fights given room (1,500–2,500+ words when the
  changing problem earns it); learning encounters and training that show effort turning
  into capability; events the current edition summarizes, put on the page; supporting
  cast given private wants, independent decisions and arcs across the formula's 8–10
  person cast; humor and warmth between people; brief POV cutaways; new episodes that
  fit canon and do not alter any book's end state.
- Shape: open-range movements of six or more chapters, one compact brief per movement,
  no per-chapter cards or per-chapter gates. Roughly 55–65 chapters of ~5,000 words per
  book in 7–9 movements; the author owns chapter boundaries.
- Rhythm: the owner-selected numerical formula (compiled into every prompt). Baseline
  of the current edition, measured by `tools/formula_metrics.py`: sentence mean ~17.5
  (target 14.6), paragraph median ~44 words (target ~18), Flesch RE ~67 (target 72.3),
  FK grade ~8.3 (target 6.8). The edition should move toward the targets.
- POV: about 87% Cael, about 13% brief purposeful cutaways spread over 4–5 named
  characters. Cutaways may never reveal what a reserved-truth boundary withholds.

## Non-negotiable

- **Reader Standard (Gate 27, owner):** written for a thirteen-year-old. Clean language
  (no profanity, obscenity, crude slang or blasphemy, including "damn", "hell" as an
  oath, "bastard" and softer cousins); moral goodness (honesty, courage, loyalty,
  restraint, care for the weak, at a cost; wrong named as wrong; cruelty never
  rewarded); violence with cost, fear and consequence and no gore; no sexual content
  or innuendo. Outranks every voice preference. See `craft/VOICE_CHARTER.md` end section.
- **Names:** keep every existing canon name. The registry's flagged collisions
  (Vell/Velmere, the -vane cluster, Wray/Greyvane, Bracken/Brom) are the owner's call
  and are NOT renamed in this edition. New minor characters the expansion needs may be
  named by the planner, screened by ear against `craft/NAME_REGISTRY.md`, and listed
  under "Names pending owner approval" in the book map. Never invent a name for an
  existing canon role.
- **Audio-first:** this ships as an audiobook. Clear referents, clear attribution,
  names distinct by ear, punctuation that exposes meaning.

## Where things live

- Canon (read-only for this edition): `universe/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md`,
  `universe/CANON_RULES.md`, `universe/STATE_LEDGER.md`, `craft/NAME_REGISTRY.md`,
  `craft/VOICE_CHARTER.md`, `series/THE_FRACTURED_PATH_SERIES.md`.
- Source edition (the story being retold): `books/<book>/CHAPTER_ARCHITECTURE.md` and
  `books/<book>/chapters/chapter-NN.md`. Read them for events, not for sentences.
- This edition: `editions/monroe-1.3/<book>/` — `BOOK_MAP.md`, `STATE_LEDGER.md`,
  `packets/MOVEMENT-NNN.md`, `manuscript/chapter-NN.md`, `state/movement-NNN/`.
- Series-level edition maps: `editions/monroe-1.3/SERIES_MAP.md`, `CHARACTERS.md`.
- Metrics: `python3 editions/monroe-1.3/tools/formula_metrics.py <chapters...>`.

## Rhythm calibration (measured, 2026-10-01)

Book 1 Movement 1 — the first movement drafted from this brief — overshot the formula in
the short direction: sentence mean 8.8 words (target 14.6), 41% of sentences at five
words or fewer (target ~28%), 0.3% at forty-plus (target ~3.3%), Flesch-Kincaid grade 2.8
(target 6.8), about 106 dialogue tags per 10k words (target ~41). Paragraph median (15)
and scene spacing were close. So, when drafting:

- Let thought, action and description run in full, well-built sentences. Compound and
  complex sentences with a clear hierarchy are the house texture, not the exception.
- Join a run of three or four clipped statements into one sentence when they are one thought.
- Keep short sentences for beats that land — a recognition, a hit, a turn — not as default.
- Give each chapter a few deliberately long, readable sentences (forty words or more)
  where an action or a thought earns the length.
- Drop "he said" / "she said" when the paragraph already makes the speaker clear.
- Keep short paragraphs (median ~18 words) and scene breaks about every 950 words.

## No scripted prose surgery (2026-10-01)

Rhythm and length repairs are done by reading, sentence by sentence, never by a script that
splits sentences or paragraphs at clause or sentence boundaries. Two repairs did that and
each needed a recheck to find the splits that broke a thought. Where the formula and a
character's speech disagree, keep the speech: one-to-three-word dialogue lines and the short
landing beats of a fight are not drift to be repaired.

## Working ranges and accepted drift (coordinator, 2026-10-01, after five repairs)

Five movement repairs show three of the formula's numbers pulling against the brief's other
asks (full sentences as house texture, speech kept as people speak, paragraphs broken where the
thought turns). Repairs that chase one of them push another out. So, until the owner rules
otherwise, these are the working ranges a movement is held to, and repairs aim at them — not at
the decimals:

| Measure | Formula target | Working range | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sentence mean | 14.6 | 13–15.5 | primary target — keep |
| ≥40-word share | 3.3% | 2.5–4.5% | primary target — keep |
| Words per scene | ~950 | 850–1,050 | primary target — keep |
| ≤5-word share | 27.7% | up to ~34% | dialogue-heavy movements; short speech is kept |
| Paragraph median | ~18 | up to ~30 | the 18 is an ASR pause proxy, not print; long cutaways run longer |
| Flesch-Kincaid grade | 6.8 | 3.5–6 | clear short words; the syllable estimator is approximate |

Measured drift outside a working range is reported in the movement's review; a repair is for
the primary three and for concrete defects.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — editions/monroe-1.3/book-01-the-shattered/BOOK_MAP.md

# BOOK MAP — The Fractured Path, Book 1: The Shattered (Monroe 1.3 edition)

Seat: `oconnor` 1.3.0 · Foundation: Monroe Jackson 1.3.0 · Public byline: Monroe Jackson
Requested author: Opus (Claude Opus 5.5) · Planning seat: Opus · Status: PLAN — no manuscript prose exists
Source edition retold: `books/book-01-the-shattered/` (24 chapters, ~105k words of usable prose; `chapter-01-revised-draft.md` is not part of the run and was not used)
Target: **~300,000 words · 60 chapters · 9 movements**

Canon governs facts; owner direction and the numerical formula govern shape. Where this map
and canon disagree, canon wins and the disagreement goes back to the owner (see §11).

---

## 1. Entry state and ending state (binding)

### Entry (Movement 1, chapter 1 — the morning of the day before Kindling)

- **Cael** (formally Caelen Hesk-ward, registration 41-7843-V) is fourteen — the birthday was
  days ago, in autumn, and the Kindling certification appointment was booked by the city a
  year in advance. Outer District of Denvash, northern quadrant, 14 Fen Street. No Path, no
  tier, no Arbiter yet. Dry, observant, a counter of things, a keeper of notebooks (three, the
  current one with eleven descriptions of Kindling on page seven, Hesk's starred).
- **Hesk**, his grandfather (about 54: he was 22 "thirty-two years" before the book), Iron-tier,
  instrument-maker, registration 22-1190-H, has known for "a few years" that Cael's
  architecture looked wrong. Cael's preliminary assessment read "irregular coupling patterns" /
  "uncommon variation." Hesk has researched the provisions and has a leather notebook ready.
- Cael already knows the public registry summary's four-line [SHATTERED] entry ("four
  instances"). He does **not** know the archive's dispositions or Hesk's history.

### Ending (Movement 9, chapter 60 — about seven months later, late spring)

- Cael is still **fourteen** (no autumn crossed; no birthday staged). He lives at **Torvin's**
  boarding house in Ardenmere's Unranked District (a shared room). Lira has her own small room
  two streets over. (Book 2 opens a year after arrival with both in two adjoining rooms near
  the Ironyard — that move happens between books.)
- **Fragments: two**, both partial and unnamed — Wind-adjacent (source: Lira; notice at
  Torvin's during the six weeks) and Pressure-adjacent (source: Feryn; notice that night after
  the Feryn bout; Cael's working hypothesis is contact). **First concurrent use** in the
  Darrow bout's fourth exchange, followed by the "Concurrent function: recorded" notice. No
  third fragment. The Arbiter has been dark since the word.
- **Circuit standing:** assessed-Copper (Vell's informal Copper R5–6 equivalent); Vell's
  ledger lists him **by name** for the first time — def. Darrow Innes (Bronze R1, Iron Path).
  The district would notice if he vanished.
- **Body:** deep bruising right side rib-to-hip after Darrow (a week down at least; something
  in the ribs "sounded like a struck beam"), a visual wobble at the edges fading over the
  evening; older bruises healed.
- **Compact:** legal review pending after Ilsev's senior evaluation (months). Coss has found a
  senior-level flag on the file he cannot read and closed it without a note (Book 6 plant).
  Cael does not know about that flag. He knows about Ilsev's unrecognized flag.
- **Open, unresolved, and must stay so:** the market stranger (unidentified, patient, not
  Compact, "interested"); who "someone else" is; Hesk's name and the fourth case's end;
  Coss's exact rank; Lira's life before her academy; the mechanism of the borrowing.
- **People:** Hesk in Denvash, writing; Lira, partner, forgave the wager misstep once and said
  it does not renew automatically; Feryn's open account ("When you figure it out, I want to
  know."); Darrow gone on his tour; Vell, Torvin, the old yard-owner, Renn, Dessa known.
- **Not in this book:** Brom, Karis, Greyvane, Quenna, any academy offer, Dace and the Ironyard,
  Havel, the Level 4 designation, Fenmark by name, [UNBOUND], the Power Log as a separate
  volume (in Book 1 the log is one notebook with a private back section).

## 2. The spine — every major source event, in order (all KEEP)

Order and outcomes are canon. The new edition may add scenes between these; it may not drop,
reorder or change the outcome of any of them. Source chapter in brackets.

1. **The day before.** Eleven descriptions, Hesk's starred. Alis Trent (Tide, Copper R1; brother
   Blade, Silver R3): "Better when it's done. Even if it's not what you wanted." — starred.
   Joren (Stone, Copper R1) after a year of certainty. The registry summary's four-line entry.
   Rooftop: Hesk researched "provisions"; the preliminary assessment; he has seen it once. Radio:
   an Ardenmere Wind practitioner dropped her stance before the commitment. "Ask Hesk about
   Tuesdays." "I know how it started. I don't know how it ended." 2 a.m.: *whatever it is, we
   figure it out.* [1]
2. **Kindling.** Weaver's Row certification office (an old dye-house). Twins, the glazed boy
   (Force). Warden-Adjunct Pellin. The circle; the sigil from behind the sternum; **eleven
   seconds**; **[SHATTERED]**; the brass plate lights eleven seconds late; the rarely used form;
   the phone; the side room and the angled tree; thirty-seven minutes; Hesk's prepared stillness;
   "a few years"; what Hesk believes; **"Don't let anyone see you move… Not until you know what
   you're doing."**; "start paying attention to what you don't decide"; Alis's raised hand; the
   careful tea. [2]
3. **The Record.** Expulsion notice, second morning (Statute 14, Section 9; 14 Fen Street;
   22-1190-H). "They're not expelling me. They're expelling you. From having me." The archive's
   full entry: three dead within weeks ("practice-related incident"), one unknown, last update
   32 years ago. Hesk's confession: Compact regional logistics at 22, saw the file opened, asked
   twice, mustered out; a name he will not yet give. Packing; the three gifts (money in small
   denominations, the leather notebook — not until the road, Ardenmere); "You're not behind.
   You're early." [3]
4. **Exit.** Ressa ("Safe travels"); Garrik looks away; the six o'clock light; the depot farewell
   ("Write to me… Especially then." / "You were worth raising before any of it"); one look back;
   the Outer-gate guard waves him through faster; the gate entry and its corollary; the road;
   Hesk's notebook, entries First–Fifth. [4]
5. **Ardenmere.** Waystation; the city from the rise; the open gate; Torvin's ROOMS, shared room;
   Yeni; the market; the district's geography (residential core, river workshops, practitioners'
   quarter, registry-free market, the information seller); the old man in the yard ("come back
   with coin for yard time"); the notice board at the crossroads. [5]
6. **Lira.** "You've been reading that board for eleven minutes." Her expulsion (a better
   deflection variant demonstrated in an exam). Copper R2, Wind. Cael says [SHATTERED] himself.
   "Can you fight?" vs "have you fought". The cart and the skewer vendor. "The gap." Cinder House
   tomorrow. Log: *question instead of a verdict.* [6]
7. **The Rules.** Lira explains the circuit (Ledger-keeper, no-kill as social fact, ratings,
   unrated, Vell, the Ash Path fighter, Marrow). The grappler beats the Blade fighter; the Bronze
   pair. Lira offers to vouch. [7]
8. **First bout — Renn** (Copper R3, Blade, 11 fights, 8 wins). Vell's terms said back. Four
   exchanges; the heel tell; the third exchange's unaccountable evasion of the herding figure;
   down in the fourth. "It doesn't miss. It missed." "Ask Vell to put us down again. A month
   out." Vell's long entry; "You lasted longer than the book said you would." "You almost had
   him…" / "I wasn't trying to win…" / "Oh. That's actually smarter." "Instinct." [8]
9. **Pattern.** Three more unrated losses (Brenna, Shield R2; Amrit Sole, Ember R4; Petra Voss,
   Force R3), each longer. The Log begins (entries incl. Baro, watched). Mornings with Lira; "not
   a natural… the most efficient learner." **Book 3 plant:** the low sweeping evasion in
   sparring — "How did you know that was coming?" / "I don't know." Becoming noticed. Vell's
   private note. [9]
10. **The weight.** The Arbiter attempt in Torvin's courtyard: dark, absence not silence. Lira:
    checking it is "like checking a ledger"; she looked the word up after day one; first time it
    is a shared, present problem; she reads the notice; **"The circuit doesn't care what the
    registry says."** [10]
11. **The Compact.** About five weeks in: a man with the Compact pin walks the market. Torvin's
    "four in eleven years". The yard-owner: "Wonder what changed." Lira's arithmetic. Letter to
    Hesk. **Book 6 plant:** the sweep is not standard procedure. [11]
12. **First win — Dessa** (Copper R5, Stone). Two weeks watching her from the benches; the late
    back-foot tell; the third exchange built and earned; the fourth exchange's unchosen pivot.
    "No book on you." "Rematch?" "No. Not yet." Vell: "It's a compliment. Don't spend it." The
    promise: Lira first, before Hesk. Doss's nod; Torvin's speech. [12]
13. **The Notice — Coss** (POV). Eleven years, forty-one; the thin file; the sweep routed above
    his clearance; Halvern; Torvin's minimum; the circuit missed; the first "physical retrieval"
    clause he has written for a child; the risky report note. Summons delivered. "Forty-eight
    hours. Find the seam." [13]
14. **The Summons.** Halden's archive; the cross-reference (standard compliance needs a
    recognized classification; [SHATTERED] isn't; senior authorization required); Lira cuts the
    defensive clause; Coss concedes; closed-pending; six weeks. "Get stronger. Build a reason for
    people to notice if I disappear." [14]
15. **Six weeks.** Six bouts, four wins (a Copper R2; a prepared opponent; **Sarel** Bronze R1
    Blade, loss; **Talis** Copper R4 Stone, ground-vibration tell, loss; **Dellin** Copper R3
    Force; **Corbin** Copper R3, veteran). "Gone." Cael reads a habit in Lira's fighting. Lira's
    own room. **First fragment notice**, thirty-ninth night, at Torvin's. The clumsy deliberate
    step. **Assessed-Copper** ("For what it's worth. I do."). [15]
16. **What the notebook says.** Two weeks of private theory; the **Kestrel** experiment
    (inconclusive); **he tells Lira** (ratified canon: from here she is a knowing participant);
    the Feryn bout offered; "So I need three more weeks."; the three-name list; **Corvane**
    (watch the build, not the release). [16]
17. **Bronze — Feryn** (Bronze R2, Pressure, ~24, touring). Two interim wins. Feryn: "He's a
    child." Consent asked of Cael. Four exchanges: the roof of air; the shed angle; the
    Wind step around the release; thrown with his own speed, the grip held one beat. Vell's call.
    "[SHATTERED]." Appetite, not fear. Dinner. **"When you figure it out, I want to know."**
    **Pressure notice** that night; contact hypothesis; Kestrel reread. [17]
18. **Senior evaluation — Ilsev.** Cael finds the temporary category before dawn and builds the
    answer; it holds; legal review (months). Coss's frustration through the wall. **Book 6
    plant:** the flag Ilsev did not request and cannot trace. He enters a bout for next week. [18]
19. **The district.** A quiet week; recognized without being explained; "Unclassified."; the
    apricots; Lira on trust; Torvin holds the rent; Sella named; Hesk: "You're describing a home."
    [19]
20. **Coss again.** Plain clothes, a public table: policy, not law; leave voluntarily; "I can
    make it disappear"; the flag question and his stillness. Lira: someone else wants him gone.
    He is not leaving. Log: *He doesn't want me gone. Someone else wants me gone. He's
    delivering the message.* [20]
21. **The Wager.** Visibility as friction. Vell's three conditions (she vetoes the opponent; no
    special terms; Lira told before anyone signs). Feryn names **Darrow Innes**. Lira's anger;
    the repair; "This forgiveness doesn't renew automatically." "You're more like him than you
    probably realize." [21]
22. **Preparation.** Precision that accumulates; the padded post; Feryn on futility; Hesk's
    letter (the timepiece; **"Being seen is not the same as being safe. But it's not
    nothing."**); the steps conversation ("It changes who'd have to explain themselves");
    Coss's second visit, less composed; "I can't out-quiet that."; his daughter. [22]
23. **The Bout.** ~300 witnesses; odds 11→13→15→13 at the close; Feryn's coat; the crossing;
    four exchanges; the Wind evasion shown in public in the third; the fourth: Wind and Pressure
    together, force sent back up its own road; the tenth strike; Darrow sits down; Vell: "It goes
    in the book… as it happened."; "What are you?" / "I don't fully know yet."; Lira's fear
    after; the concurrent notice; the closing line. [23]
24. **The Message.** The ledger by name; the district's protection; Hesk's "I'm proud of you"
    letter; **Coss (POV): the senior-level flag**, closed without a note; **the market
    stranger**, two days after the fight; Lira: not alone; the final log lines. [24]

## 3. The expansion plan — where ~190k new words come from

Every addition below fits inside the spine, changes no end state, and discloses nothing ahead
of schedule. Approximate shares of the 300k: spine retold at scene pace ~150k; developed
fights +45k; learning/training arcs +35k; summarized events on the page +20k; supporting-cast
arcs and community +25k (partly carried by cutaways); POV cutaways 39k (overlaps the above).

### 3a. Fights given room (largest single expansion, ~45k net)

Source Book 1 develops about seven bouts; three are pure summary and six more are a line
each. The new edition puts roughly twenty contests on the page, sized to their changing
problem (the 1,500–2,500+ allowance is a ceiling for a real problem, not a quota):

| Contest | Movement | Allowance | Why it earns the room |
|---|---|---|---|
| Joren and Cael at the low wall (friendly, Stone Copper R1, pre-Kindling for Cael) | M1 | 800–1,200 | Teaches what a fresh declaration feels like from the outside; Joren's patience; Cael's watching before he has anything |
| The grappler vs the Blade fighter; the Bronze pair (watched) | M2 | 1,500 combined | The first "trade a small wound for distance" lesson; reading a fight as homework |
| **Renn I** | M2 | 2,000–2,500 | First real fight; the heel tell bought with a shoulder; the unaccountable third exchange |
| Brenna; Amrit Sole; Petra Voss (all losses) | M3 | 1,200–2,000 each | Three different problems (pressure, heat tell, speed); each loss longer and more informative — the reader learns the method by watching it fail usefully |
| Morning sparring incl. the plant | M3 | 1,000–1,500 | The Book 3 plant needs an exchange the reader can see |
| **Dessa I** | M4 | 2,500 | First win; patience against a patience fighter; built third exchange vs unbuilt fourth |
| Lira's bout (new, on Coss's second afternoon) | M5 | 1,500–2,000 | Lira's own competence; Cael's months of watching her accumulate on the page; the Coss irony staged three streets away |
| Six-week bouts: the Copper R2 opener; **Renn II**; **Sarel**; Talis; Dellin; **Corbin** | M6 | 800 / 2,000 / 2,500 / 1,500 / 1,200 / 2,000 | The fight movement: watching vs speed (Sarel), a tell he wasn't looking for (Talis), the plan that survives (Dellin), the measuring-stick win (Corbin), the rival who came back with a plan (Renn) |
| Two interim wins | M7 | 600–900 each | Unremarkable competence as its own milestone |
| **Feryn** | M7 | 3,000 | The book's midpoint set piece; reading improves and is still outrun; the contact |
| **Dessa II** (the bout he enters after Ilsev — summarized in source, on the page here) | M8 | 1,500–2,000 | Pays her "Not yet… then we'll talk"; tests whether his reading survives a defender who now knows him; outcome author's choice |
| The old yard-owner's lesson (no Path shown) | M8 | 1,000–1,500 | A learning fight with an old Iron-tier body: stance, patience, how a veteran spends nothing |
| **Darrow** | M9 | 5,000–6,000 across two chapters | The climax: futility as a weapon, accumulation over power, the crossing, the concurrent use |

Carry every injury forward (see §6 and the ledger). A loss may buy knowledge; it is never a
disguised win.

### 3b. Learning and training arcs (~35k)

- **Hesk's workshop (M1).** Load calculations, "never trust a number you can't trace back to
  where it came from," a housing that fails its stress test. Cael's whole method begins here;
  show it as a craft he already has.
- **The community hall on Tuesdays (M1, new).** Pays the source's planted note "ask Hesk about
  Tuesdays": Hesk takes him once to the broadcast of circuit and Path athletics; they watch a
  fight together and Hesk shows him how to watch a stance. A craft lesson, a grandfather's secret
  hobby, and the origin of Cael's attention to "the third exchange".
- **Lira's curriculum (M3–M9, every movement).** A continuing syllabus, not repeated drills:
  her three-part deflection and the skipped middle; "the gap"; thinking about feet until you
  stop; the shaded half of the yard; "gone" said aloud until the letting-go needs no word;
  demonstrating his mistake back at him; finally precision that accumulates (Iron Path). Cael
  teaches back: he names a habit she did not know she had.
- **The Log as method (M3 on).** Entries, columns of claim/evidence/ruling, the private back
  section written only after the tactical entries. Teaching the log is teaching the progression
  engine of the whole series.
- **Corvane (M7).** An afternoon becomes a lesson with a body in it: she makes him stand while
  she "builds" and asks what he sees; the stick figure and the spiral.
- **Deliberate fragment practice (M6–M9).** Clumsy on purpose, reliable only under pressure;
  the step in the narrow space by the cot; sitting an hour with the texture; Pressure tried
  alone, slow and visible. Crude costs only (see §6 limits).
- **Iron Path precision (M9).** The padded post that records nothing; three, four, five strikes
  to a place "that hasn't finished healing yet."

### 3c. Summarized events put on the page (~20k)

- The road: the cart he stepped from before seeing it (source mentions it once, unwritten), the
  waystation woman, the stray dog. [M1]
- Hesk's first week alone and the guild-standing review the notice threatened (Hesk cutaways).
- The six weeks' bouts (all six), the two interim wins before Feryn, the bout after Ilsev.
- Lira's saving for and taking her own room. [M5–M6]
- The Kestrel watching days and the failed summonings. [M7]
- The three-name list hunt: the fighter gone to the coast; the woman whose lesson was "don't";
  Lira's negotiation with Corvane. [M7]
- Letters both ways, with the lag shown (§8).
- The quiet week: the errand boys' heavy delivery day, the yard-owner's afternoon, Torvin's
  table with the boarders. [M8]
- The district turning out for Darrow: slates, errand boys, the baker shutting her stall. [M9]

### 3d. Supporting cast — private wants and independent decisions (8–10 people)

Formula: many small beats each (0.1–0.7 per 10k per character), not one deuteragonist taking
the whole secondary budget. Suggested beat counts over the book in brackets.

| Character | Private want | Independent decisions on the page | Beats |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Lira** (15, Wind, Copper R2) | To be acknowledged for doing it her own better way; a room of her own | Vouches (spends standing); trains him by her method; chooses her own bout; takes her own room; confronts him over the wager and sets a term; "During, I coach. After, I get to be a person." | 16–20 |
| **Hesk** (~54, Iron, instrument-maker) | That Cael be safe and that he himself be honest at last | Chooses Cael over his standing; faces the guild review alone; decides what to put in letters and what to leave out; keeps the name (OPEN) | 10–12 |
| **Vell** (50s, retired Bronze, Ledger-keeper) | An honest ledger, a circuit that survives itself | Matches him against better opposition; writes notes procedure does not require; enters Feryn's objection; the call against Feryn; three conditions on the marquee | 8–10 |
| **Coss** (41, Compact field agent) | That procedure actually protect people; a daughter Cael's age | The risky report note; concedes correctly; off-record warnings; closes the flagged file without a note | 8–10 |
| **Feryn** (~24, Bronze R2, Pressure) | To be surprised; his own evaluation next season | Objects, then accepts Cael's consent; buys dinner; keeps an open account; finds Darrow; bets at eleven | 6–8 |
| **Torvin** | A quiet, profitable house; privately decent | Tells the Compact the legal minimum; holds the rent; "Glad I was wrong about the not-much part." | 3–4 |
| **The old yard-owner** (unnamed by design; retired Iron-tier) | Serious people; to be left his past | "Wonder what changed"; the lesson; the one-degree nod | 3–4 |
| **Renn** (~20, Blade, Copper R3; carried freight) | The second look | Books the rematch; comes back with a plan; theatrical insistence he "let" Cael win | 3–4 |
| **Dessa** (~20, Stone, Copper R5) | To watch him from the benches for once | Refuses the rematch "not yet"; comes back for it on her schedule | 2–3 |
| **Ilsev** (~40, Assessor) | To get the regulation right | Concedes; notes the flag anyway; tells him the truth incomplete | 2–3 |

Lighter threads (a beat or two each, no POV): Alis and Joren (seen in Hesk's cutaways — Joren
working at patience, Alis at the healers'), Ressa, Garrik, Yeni, Doss, Sella and the brothers,
the errand boys, the baker who never gave her name twice, the dried-fruit woman, Halden,
Halvern, Corvane, Kestrel, Darrow.

### 3e. New episodes (each listed with its reason)

1. **Joren at the low wall (M1).** The friend who was certain, now beginning; teaches a fresh
   declaration from outside, before Cael has anything. Witnessing does not transfer (his
   architecture is not yet active; and see the Kestrel limit).
2. **The Tuesday broadcast (M1).** Pays a planted note; shows where Cael learned to watch.
3. **Hesk's guild review (Hesk cutaways).** The notice's threat made real at a cost Hesk
   chose; never resolves into disaster on the page (outcome PROVISIONAL — keep it pending or
   quietly survived; do not strip his standing, as later books show him working).
4. **Lira's bout on Coss's afternoon (M5).** Her competence on the page; the circuit as a
   venue Coss's training cannot see.
5. **Buying one answer from the information seller (M4 or M5, optional).** Cael spends coin to
   learn what the Compact man has been asking about — agency, and the district's economy of
   knowledge. Answer must stay inside what a local could know (that a Compact agent asked after
   a boy at Torvin's; nothing about flags).
6. **Renn II (M6).** Gives the source's unnamed "prepared opponent" a canon face and pays
   Renn's booking.
7. **Dessa II (M8).** Gives the unnamed post-Ilsev bout a canon face and pays her "Then we'll
   talk."
8. **The yard-owner's lesson (M8).** A veteran's body teaching economy; keeps him unnamed and
   his Path undemonstrated.

## 4. Movements

Nine movements, sixty chapters at ~5,000 words. The author owns chapter boundaries inside
each range. No movement exceeds 42,000 words.

| # | Chapters | Words | Source retold | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | 1–6 (6) | 30,000 | 1–4 (+ road from 5) | Feel the expulsion; teach the Path system hard; a home worth losing |
| M2 | 7–13 (7) | 35,000 | 5–8 | Arrive, find Lira and the circuit, lose to Renn and learn something nobody saw |
| M3 | 14–20 (7) | 35,000 | 9–10 | Losing usefully; the Log; the Book 3 plant; the dark Arbiter becomes a shared problem |
| M4 | 21–27 (7) | 35,000 | 11–12 | The Compact's shadow arrives; two weeks of watching buy the first win |
| M5 | 28–33 (6) | 30,000 | 13–14 | Coss's view of a file too thin; forty-eight hours to find the seam |
| M6 | 34–40 (7) | 35,000 | 15 | The six weeks — fight-heavy; the first fragment notice; assessed-Copper |
| M7 | 41–47 (7) | 35,000 | 16–17 | Telling Lira; preparing for Bronze; Feryn; the second door |
| M8 | 48–53 (6) | 28,000 | 18–20 | Ilsev and the flag; Dessa II; the quiet week; Coss off the record; the choice to stay |
| M9 | 54–60 (7) | 37,000 | 21–24 | Loud on purpose: the wager, the repair, Darrow, the flag, the stranger |
| **Total** | **60** | **300,000** | 24 | |

Book thirds by volume: first third ≈ chapters 1–20 (M1–M3), middle ≈ 21–40 (M4–M6), last ≈
41–60 (M7–M9).

Every movement has a physical challenge or learning fight (§3a). M8 contains the deliberately
quiet week; it keeps Dessa II and the yard-owner's lesson so the movement still moves.

## 5. POV plan

**Cael ~87% (~261,000 words), close third.** Cutaways ~13% (~39,000) over four named
characters at 3.0–3.7% each — brief, purposeful, never alternating chapters, never at a fight's
decisive exchange.

| Cutaway | Share | Where | Allowed to know | Must not know / reveal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Coss** | ~3.7% (~11k) | M5 (~6k: the file, Halvern, Torvin, walking the district, the summons); M8 (~2.5k: Ilsev's report, his own frustration, the decision to go off record); M9 (~2.5k: the senior flag) | Standard procedure and how far this file departs from it; the sweep's untraceable regional authorization; Ilsev's flag (from her report); the senior-level flag's existence and coding; his daughter; by M8 that the boy fights in the circuit | Who or what set any flag; anything about the Arbiter's nature, the Architect, falsification, the fourth case's end, the fragments' mechanism. He may suspect "someone above"; never a non-human source. His exact rank stays OPEN — do not state it |
| **Lira** | ~3.3% (~10k) | M3 (after the plant: what she saw and decided not to push); M6 (the six weeks from her side; the room; her habit named by him); M7 (being told "some of it's yours"); M9 (the night after the wager, deciding to stay in) | What she has seen and what Cael has told her; her own academy story as given in source; her Path and its feel | The mechanism (she has hypotheses, as he does); her family or life before the academy (OPEN); the academy's name (Fenmark is first named in Book 2); no romance |
| **Hesk** | ~3.0% (~9k) | M2 (the house after; the review notice; Ressa and Garrik; the first letter); M6 (letters about the sweep and summons; Joren and Alis call; the review); M8 (writing the "being seen" letter in answer to Cael's earlier question) | Denvash; his workshop; what letters tell him, a week late; the beginning of the fourth case exactly as he told it in source | The name, the fourth practitioner's fate, anything more of his history (reserved for a later book — Hesk thinks *around* it, never through it); the Architect; flags beyond what Cael writes |
| **Vell** | ~3.0% (~9k) | M2 (the ledger entry after Renn); M4 (matching Dessa; the private note); M7 (Feryn walks in and sees a child); M9 (calling Darrow and entering it "as it happened") | Thirty years of the circuit; her ratings; what she has watched; "nothing to register"; [SHATTERED] once Cael says it to Feryn at her table | The mechanism; any old archive or pre-standardization material (Book 2's thread and the [UNBOUND] note are Book 2/8 material — do not touch); Brom, Dace, the Ironyard |

POV discipline: a cutaway may plant or deepen; it may never answer a reserved question.

## 6. Progression plan

### The system's vocabulary (front-loaded — formula §4)

Book 1's progression vocabulary is the Path system itself plus the circuit's ladder: Kindling,
Arbiter, sigil, Path, declaration, classification, registry, tier (Copper/Iron/Bronze/Silver/
Gold/Platinum/Void), Rank 1–10, evaluation, district access, unrated, circuit rating,
assessed-Copper, Ledger, exchange, fragment notice, "-adjacent", integration, tier equivalent.

Target ~58 hits per 10k words over the book, shaped like the source formula (900 / 471 / 514
by thirds): **first third ~83/10k, middle ~44/10k, last ~48/10k.** Teach it hardest in
M1–M3: other people's declarations (Joren shows his — bravado; the Bible's standard format),
the tiers and city access by rank, the circuit's unofficial ladder, Lira's Arbiter check, every
opponent introduced by Path, tier, rank and record. After M3 the system surfaces at turning
points: ratings (M6), notices (M6, M7, M9), the legal categories (M5, M8).

Any invented declaration text must follow the Bible's standard format
(`PATH DECLARATION — COPPER RANK 1` / `[Name] — …`). New ability labels are new canon nouns:
keep them few and flag them in the movement's state report for approval.

### Cael's capabilities — where, how, at what cost

| Movement | What appears or changes | Cost / limit on the page |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | Watching as craft (workshop, broadcast, Joren). The road: he steps from a cart before seeing it — unwritten until Lira's question in M2 makes him remember it | Nothing explains it; Hesk's "pay attention to what you don't decide" |
| M2 | Renn's third exchange: off the herding figure's lane with no read in it. He files it "whole and unexamined" | Owns none of the answering skill — his one strike is swatted; the shoulder, the ribs |
| M3 | **The Book 3 plant** in sparring with Lira — the low sweeping evasion that is not his; "How did you know that was coming?" / "I don't know." Arbiter dark | Unrepeatable on call; the wrong coat; private dread |
| M4 | Dessa: the built third exchange (his) and the unchosen pivot in the fourth (not his) | Leaves nothing behind; hands shaking |
| M6 | **Wind-adjacent notice** (thirty-ninth night of the six weeks, at Torvin's — Book 2 places it there). He makes the step on purpose, badly | Clumsy and slow on demand; reliable only under pressure; breath and hip simply "cost something" he does not yet itemize |
| M7 | The step around Feryn's release in the third exchange; **Pressure-adjacent notice** that night after the held grip | Fourth exchange: called for, it does not come; thrown with his own speed; forearm, ribs, the fall |
| M8 | Practice: the Wind step steadier; Pressure tried alone — slow (a full breath), visible in his silhouette, a leaden arm afterwards | Not dependable; "only ever used alone and in fragments" before Darrow |
| M9 | Wind shown in public in the third exchange (a cost to visibility he budgeted against); the **concurrent use** in the fourth: the angle and the absorption together, some force turned back up Darrow's road. The notice: *Concurrent function: recorded.* | Ribs, a week down; the wobble; the knowledge that he did not do it — it happened in him |

**Instance count — fix the source's inconsistency.** Cael's log must count coherently. He
refuses to examine Renn (M2) and calls the sparring plant his "first instance" (M3); at the
Dessa entry (M4) he writes "second instance" and, for the first time, sorts the Renn stone into
the count honestly. Drop the source's duplicate "It happened again today" second sparring
episode.

**Fragment limits that belong to Book 2 (do not pre-empt):** the itemized Wind costs — the held
half-breath as a named price, the locked **landing beat** (Lira discovers it in Book 2's winter
testing), the per-exchange ceiling, a "functional deployment range" column; the Pressure
fragment's measured drain. In Book 1 the costs are felt and roughly logged, not itemized. The
log stays one notebook with a private back section (Book 2 separates the fragment log).

### Development cadence (formula §5)

Cael: ~4.5 growth/realization beats per 10k (~135 over the book; ~38 / 51 / 46 by thirds — a
slight middle bump, never saved for the finale). About one every 2,200 words. Supporting cast:
per §3d.

No conscious, intentional absorption; no rule stated; Kestrel stays inconclusive. One plant required — M3 sparring (plus Renn and Dessa as support) |

| Arbiter = interface; Architect; its will; the reset | B9 / B8 / B11 / B13 | No character theorises a maker, a system intelligence or a non-human watcher. The stranger is a person. "Above the Compact's ordinary chain" is as far as Cael's guess goes |
| The four prior cases were eliminated on instruction | — (SECRET) | Records only: three dead within weeks, one unknown. Hesk's "practice-related incident" suspicion may be voiced as doubt, not knowledge |
| Hesk's history and the name | OPEN (later book) | Exactly what he says in source Ch3, no more — including in his cutaways |

## 9. Prose targets (formula, owner-selected — apply while writing, measure after)

Source baseline (`tools/formula_metrics.py`): sentence mean ~17.5, paragraph median ~44 words,
Flesch RE ~67, FK ~8.3. Move toward: **sentence mean 14.6, median 11, very short (≤5) 27.7%,
very long (≥40) 3.3%; inferred paragraph median ~18 words (mean ~26.8); scenes ~950 words
(~8.7 breaks per 10k); Flesch RE 72.3, FK 6.8**; progression vocabulary as §6; combat
vocabulary ~22 per 10k throughout, not only in fights; dialogue tags ~41 per 10k; adverbs
optional to taste; keep "that" below the source tic. Paragraph/scene values are ASR-inferred
proxies — directional, not print measurements.

Specific source habits to leave behind: the reflexive "the way he/she X'd…" simile tail on
every paragraph; the end-of-chapter lie-awake recap; "which was, he understood…"; repeated
explanations of what was just shown; the omniscient aside (source Ch11's "The chapter ends where
their knowledge ends").

Reader Standard (Gate 27) outranks all of it: no profanity or softer cousins (the source's
"Damned if I know", Ch5, does not carry), no gore, no romance or innuendo, wrong named as
wrong, cruelty never rewarded. Audio-first: clear attribution, distinct names in spoken runs
(keep Petra Voss out of any scene with Coss; never list Feryn near Oryn — not in this book
anyway; keep Vell and the -mere city apart in a sentence where possible).

**None required.** The expansion is built from existing canon names and from deliberately
unnamed roles (the old yard-owner — unnamed by design and must stay so; the baker who gave her
name once; the errand boys; the information seller; the waystation keeper; Torvin's wife; the
wheezing man; the brothers; the twins; the glazed boy). Use role placeholders if a role needs
a handle.

Optional, only if the Hesk cutaway needs the Denvash guild reviewer named:
- **Ebbe** — [GUILD REVIEWER], Denvash. By ear: EB-eh. No Eb- name in Books 1–8; distinct from
  Ephram (EF-rum) and Edran (ED-ran) by consonant and syllable stress; not near the reserved
  list (Eli is EE-lie). Otherwise leave as "the reviewing officer".

## 11. Canon conflicts found in the source (for the owner)

1. **Series bible vs Book 1:** "The last time it appeared, eleven people died" (Series
   Identity) contradicts Book 1, the ledger and later books: four recorded instances, three
   dead within weeks, one unknown. The plan follows Book 1.
2. **Pellin's sex:** prose (Ch2) — a woman of about fifty; architecture — "he"; **Book 6 Ch9**
   — "A man in an office decided what I was in eleven seconds." The plan keeps the Book 1 prose;
   Book 6 needs a one-word owner decision.
3. **Darrow's weakness:** Book 1 Ch23's "crossing" (load road, low left rib-to-hip) vs Book 3
   Ch8's recollection, "Darrow Innes's evasion-reset vulnerability." Reconcile by writing the
   crossing as the half-beat his frame needs to reset after redistributing a load.
4. **Series bible key scene / ending:** "Hesk walking Cael out through the back exit" (source:
   side room, then home by the street) — reconcilable: Hesk takes him out the back. "Wins his
   first real public fight" — Darrow is not his first win (Dessa is); read it as first marquee.
5. **Internal seams in the source** (fix quietly, no canon change): Vell introduced and the
   vouching staged twice (Ch8); the instance count (Ch8/Ch9/Ch12); Ch10's "He waited four days"
   and a summons that does not yet exist; Ch11 attributes "being seen…" to Hesk at the depot
   before the Ch22 letter coins it; Ch19's log quotes "someone else wants me gone" before Ch20
   coins it; Coss says he first read the file "in Denvash" (Ch20) vs Ardenmere (Ch13); Renn's
   tell "quarter second" (Ch8) vs "half a second" (Ch9 log); letter transit "days" vs "three or
   four weeks"; Lira's embrace after Darrow (Ch24) that Ch23 never shows; Ch8's quotation of a
   Hesk line Ch4 never wrote.
6. **Architecture vs LOCKED timeline:** Ch1 card says "age 13 (one day before Kindling)"; prose
   and canon say fourteen. Fourteen holds.
7. **Reader Standard breach in source:** "Damned if I know" (Ch5).

Entry state for the book, from canon and the source edition. Append one section per movement
after it is drafted, from the surviving prose (not from the plan). Where prose and plan differ,
record the prose and note the difference. Binding end state: `BOOK_MAP.md` §1.

## ENTRY — Movement 1, chapter 1 (the morning of the day before Kindling, autumn)

### Bodies

| Who | State |
|---|---|
| Cael | Fourteen by a few days. Healthy, uninjured, thin; walks a lot; no training beyond workshop work and a lifetime of watching. Arbiter dormant (wakes in the circle tomorrow). |
| Hesk | About fifty-four. Iron-tier, retired from active practice; instrument-maker's hands; steady. |

### Knowledge

**Cael knows:** the Path system as a well-read boy knows it (Kindling at fourteen, Arbiters,
the seven tiers and ten ranks, declarations private, the Registry, district access by rank);
the public registry summary's four-line [SHATTERED] entry (four instances; "currently
deprecated") — memorised, not written down; the footnote advising non-standard results to
report for "standard compliance processing" within 48 hours (written, then crossed out); that
his preliminary assessment showed "irregular coupling patterns"; Alis's and Joren's results;
the radio's Ardenmere Wind practitioner who dropped her stance before the commitment.

**Cael does not know:** the archive's full entry (three dead within weeks; one unknown, 32
years); that Hesk has known for years; Hesk's Compact past; that Hesk goes to the community
hall on Tuesdays; anything about Ardenmere beyond the reference section.

**Hesk knows (and has not said):** the signs for "a few years"; the provisions; the archive's
dispositions; that at 22, in the Compact's regional logistics division, he saw the fourth case's
file opened; a name he has never told; a promise he made when he mustered out. (Name and end:
OPEN, reserved.)

### Resources

- Cael: three notebooks (the current one: eleven Kindling descriptions on page seven, Hesk's
  "I don't know how to describe it" starred; a five-page entry on the Inner District wall);
  a few self-made workshop tools. Registration 41-7843-V (Caelen Hesk-ward).
- Household: 14 Fen Street, Outer District, northern quadrant, Denvash; standing held by Hesk
  (22-1190-H). Hesk has a money pouch saved in small denominations and a hand-bound leather
  notebook (years of entries, several inks) — both not yet given.
- Currency: unnamed in the source; Book 2 uses (silver) marks — use marks if a unit is needed.

### Fragments and progression

- Fragments: **none**. Arbiter: not yet woken. Functional level: none; a trained eye only.
- Circuit standing: none (Ardenmere's circuit is unknown to him).

### Relationships

| With | State |
|---|---|
| Hesk | Raised him; total trust; the first small betrayal (years of silence) arrives tomorrow. |
| Alis Trent | Neighbour, two streets over; Kindled three days ago, Tide Copper R1; her brother is Blade Silver R3. Mutual respect. |
| Joren | Childhood friend; Kindled two days ago, Stone Copper R1, after a year of loud certainty; working at not looking devastated. |
| Ressa | Corner baker; has sold Hesk bread all Cael's life. |
| Garrik | Retired Copper-tier cart-fixer; taught Cael wheel bearings at eight. |
| Lira, Vell, Torvin, Coss and all Ardenmere people | Not yet met. |

### Open threads at entry

- What [SHATTERED] means (nobody living knows).
- Hesk's Tuesdays (planted in Ch1; paid in M1 if the author stages the broadcast).
- Who Hesk saw thirty-two years ago, and how it ended (OPEN beyond this book).
- The Wind practitioner's dropped stance — what she was seeing.

### Canon locks to carry through the book

- Cael is fourteen for the whole book; no birthday is staged.
- Fragment acquisitions: Wind-adjacent (Lira; notice during the six weeks, at Torvin's);
  Pressure-adjacent (Feryn; notice that night, contact hypothesis). Count at close: two, plus
  the first concurrent use. Notice texts verbatim (BOOK_MAP §7).
- Plants: Book 3 (sparring instinct, M3); Book 6 (sweep M4, authorization M5, Ilsev's flag M8,
  senior flag M9). Nothing disclosed early (BOOK_MAP §8).
- Letters: carrier post, about a week each way.

## After Movement 1 (chapters 1–6; closed after repair r1, 2026-10-01)

Source: state/movement-001/AUTHOR-REPORT.md (draft state, then the Repair r1 changes below).


**Calendar.** Day before Kindling = a Tuesday (day −1). Kindling = day 0 (Wednesday). Day 1 on the page (no events). Notice = day 2. Leaves = day 3 morning; one road night, at the waystation (night of day 3). On the rise above Ardenmere at dusk, day 4. Autumn. Cael fourteen (birthday five days before day −1).

**Bodies.** Cael: no injuries; legs stiff and sore from two days' walking; tired. Bag strap scuffed by the cart hub. Hesk: unchanged, in Denvash.

**Knowledge.**
- Cael now knows: the archive's full entry (subheading "Deprecated and Non-Standard Designations, Pre-Compact Era Holdovers", unglossed; three deceased within weeks, "practice-related incident"; one unknown, 32 years); Hesk's confession (regional logistics at 22, saw the fourth file opened, reassigned above clearance, asked twice, left 18 months later, a name and a promise, "when you're older"); that Hesk has known "a few years" and went to Tuesdays for "some years" partly to study how a Path changes a body; the stance-watching lesson (hips and feet, not hands; first exchange a handshake, second questions, third "where they tell you the truth"; a trick works once, then it's a habit someone can plan for); Joren's declaration text; Hesk's notebook entries.
- Cael does not know: the name; the fourth case's end; anything about what [SHATTERED] does. He has not examined the cart-step; he believes he must have heard the wheels.
- Joren: will have found the note (unconfirmed on page). Alis: knows the result went badly, probably not the word.

**Resources.** Hesk's money pouch (small worn coins, marks and half-marks) less one waystation night. Three notebooks (current one: eleven descriptions + two stars on page seven; "*She was right that it's done. It doesn't feel done.*" under Alis's line; Joren's declaration near the back; archive entry copied; gate entry; the rise entry). Hesk's leather notebook (in inside coat pocket) with an **unsent first letter to Hesk** folded in it — to be posted from Ardenmere once he has an address; it mentions Ressa, Garrik, the note to Joren, the notebook and Tuesdays, the carter — and **omits the cart incident**. The failed lock-gauge bracket (gift "for the drawer"). A turnip. A few self-made tools (file, off-true tweezers). Registration papers.

**Fragments & progression.** Arbiter woke at Kindling; after the word it dimmed and settled at the edge of his sight; not consulted since. No declaration, no fragments, no notices. Capability: a trained eye (stances, tells); the unexamined cart-step (instinct only, unwritten — reserved for Lira's question in M2).

**Relationships.**
- Hesk: farewell at the depot; total trust intact with the years of silence acknowledged; Cael has now made Hesk's own kind of withholding choice (the letter) and noticed it.
- Joren: Cael broke a promise to come by after Kindling (day 1); apologised and told him the word in the note in the wall gap ("Keep your heels down"). Joren owes nothing; Cael owes a letter "when I know where I am."
- Alis: mutual acknowledgment (raised hands).
- Ressa: extra loaf, unpaid.
- Garrik: warm welcome at the hall on day −1; did not look up on day 3 (turned his wheel; it stopped after they passed).
- Pellin: one look "a full second longer than she needed to."

**Open threads.** Joren's answer; the unsent letter; the cart (unwritten, unexplained); Hesk's name and the fourth case; what the Wind practitioner was seeing (her stance was anticipated and she lost the semi-final — what she saw remains open); Hesk's Tuesdays continue in Denvash (usable in Hesk cutaways); Cael's first-job plan ("get past the weeks… be still here").
